### Page 3

3
Cathay’s View市場策略思維
市場焦點
1. 市場焦點:
• 中國記憶體技術突破、通膨升溫、AI融資擔憂, 形成亞股大跌風暴: AI融資及過度擴產的擔憂甚囂塵上,
加上殖利率攀升, 提高市場評價重調並放大檢視AI利空, 形成亞股重挫主因。
• 9月通膨超預期升溫, 市場擔憂10月再升1碼: CPI月增率創5個月來新高, 美債殖利率走高, 儘管聯準會
官員暗示10月無升息意願, 但市場超前反應升息可能, 使殖利率higher-for-longer。
• Q3財報季開跑, 預估獲利年增二成以上: 金融股率先公布Q3財報, 殖利率創高削弱固定收益交易收入,
但貸款需求穩定、景氣熱絡, 6大銀行營收成長仍創近年新高, 預估Q3標普500企業獲利年增逾二成。
• 台積電財報優於預期, 強調無過度投資疑慮: Q3營收獲利優於預期, 毛利率符合預期, 但上調全年預估
營收, 並強調CSP與資料中心無過度投資或擴產疑慮, 2026-2028年資本支出將遠高於過去三年。
2. 後續看點:
• 四大CSP財報(10/27-10/28)、FOMC利率會議(10/29)、ECB政策會議(10/29)、台灣GDP(10/30)

策略思維
1. 股市−多頭不變, 但高利率增添評價波動: 殖利率震盪上揚壓力增加, 市場持續調整股市評價面, 使股價落後
基本面表現。不過, AI趨勢明確, 企業營收獲利未來成長空間仍大, 應逢評價仍低時擴大布局。
2. 債市−長端不確定性仍高, 宜以中短天期或公司債為佳: 通膨高於預期, 長率仍有上行壓力。建議控制債券部
位存續期間, 選擇短天期或浮動債較佳。
3. 匯市−美元走揚與股市震盪, 引發台幣重貶: 美元雙率走高提高外資賣超台股疑慮, 台幣可能須承受額外壓力。

Cathay’s View
債務疑慮, 驅動CoreWeave債券利率飆升 大型CSP債券利率仍偏穩定
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
• 美國9月通膨數據高於預期, 推升美債殖利率一度急彈, 進而引發市場對AI融資風險的疑慮。與此同時, 馬斯克及部
分創投人士也注意到CoreWeave債券殖利率飆升的問題, 並示警相關風險升高, 導致CoreWeave股價重挫15%。
• 不過, 對谷歌、亞馬遜、微軟等大型CSP業者而言, 儘管仍面臨現金流轉負的壓力, 但其發行的公司債價格仍相對
穩定, 並未因為CoreWeave事件而同步走高 , 反而逆勢走低 。這顯示, 目前AI融資疑慮仍主要集中在規模較小的
CSP業者, 尚未全面擴散至大型業者。然而, 隨著美債利率維持高檔, AI資本支出的融資壓力仍可能持續擾動市場。
科技｜CoreWeave債券利率飆升, 再度觸發AI融資敏感神經

### Page 6

6
Cathay’s View
• 隨美國10年公債殖利率站穩5%, 市場對AI過度投資的擔憂再起, 掀起AI拋售潮。但從產業基本面觀察, 四大CSP資
本支出仍在成長, 僅是年增率放緩, 台積電法說會亦表示沒有見到任何AI需求放緩的跡象, 意味即使AI成長放緩, 也
不代表缺少成長動能, 而是從初期萌發階段, 進入穩定成長期。
• 此外, 即便企業獲利增速放緩, 但只要能保持正成長, 普遍都可帶動指數上漲。以美台股市為例, 2021Q2為企業獲
利成長高峰, 但至2022Q3後股市才因升息與獲利轉負而下跌。近期股市大跌主要受市場重新調整估值與AI融資疑
慮影響, AI產業前景與企業基本面無虞, 逢拉回應站買方思考。

Cathay’s View
銀行股Q3財報狀況 摩根大通利息收入穩定, 但非利息收入受到債市拖累
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3財報。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026Q3財報。
• 美國6大銀行Q3獲利仍具韌性, 其中放貸需求穩定成長, 但固定收益交易動能受到拖累。以商業銀行摩根大通為例,
放款總額成長10%至1.5兆美元, 存款亦有9%的成長, 使得利息收入270億美元, 年增12%, 為近10季最強動能。不
過, 由於美債利率高漲 , 固定收益交易收入年減 4%, 使得非利息收入 Q3僅成長3%。Q3整體收入為 502億美元,
EPS為5.57美元, 不及上季, 但仍創下同期新高。
• 就投資銀行高盛而言, 股票交易收入年增72%, 但固定收益的收入也年減1%, 使得整體收入為183億美元, 雖然仍有
28%的成長, 但動能也不及上季35%。摩根史坦利亦然, 手續費收入從上季的28%降至6%。
美股｜經濟保持韌性使利息收入穩定成長, 美債利率高漲影響固定收益收入, 使銀行財報表現分化

Cathay’s View
9月油價反彈, 同步推升CPI走揚 除能源反彈強勁, 商品與服務同步上升
註: 西德州原油(WTI)採每月均值之年增率計算。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9、2026/10。
註: 超級核心通膨為服務扣除租金。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9。
• 9月CPI年增率3.7%、核心CPI年增率2.6%雙雙高於預期(3.6%/2.5%)。受美伊衝突影響, 西德州原油(WTI)月均價
由8月82.5美元/桶升至9月95.7美元/桶, 推升汽油價格月增9.1%。此外, 核心商品、核心服務月增率均較前月升高;
細項方面, 租金(+0.4%)、醫療保健(+0.3%)、運輸(+0.6%)、二手車(+0.7%)升幅較大, 通膨壓力已非侷限於能源。
• 物價壓力由「單一油價衝擊」進一步擴散至商品與服務端 , 致使整體通膨降溫進程停滯 。在通膨黏性增強 、第二
輪傳導效應持續累積下, 若美伊情勢持續緊張, 高通膨維持時間將被迫拉長, 亦使美債利率持續面臨上行壓力。

通膨｜9月CPI升幅超預期, 能源供給壓力逐漸擴散

### Page 9

Cathay’s View
CPI公布後, 升息預期先升後降 期限溢價同步推升10年美債利率
註: 聯邦利率期貨預估至2027年底約升息3碼。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/15。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
• 9月CPI超預期, 利率先升後降: 10月升息機率一度上升至50%, 2年、10年債利率一度突破4.9%、5.3%。CPI公布
後, Fed官員仍對通膨保持警戒 , 不過Bowman表示無須立即行動 、Daly認為無需倉促決定升息 、Goolsbee與
Barkin傾向觀察更多數據; 官員談話緩和連續升息疑慮, 牽動10月升息機率回落至27%, 美債殖利率自高點回落。
• 通膨壓力未解, 升息預期將給予短端利率支撐; 長端利率則進一步受日漸墊高的期限溢價影響, 推升利率中樞水平
上移。中長期而言, 除了升息路徑的不確定性, 在財政赤字惡化、AI融資推升發債需求的背景下, 高利率環境仍將
成為市場常態。
利率｜短線升息預期反覆, 惟長端利率中樞仍偏高

高利率推升民生信用壓力
註: 嚴重拖欠率係指90天以上逾期(90+Delinquency)。
資料來源: Bloomberg, NY Fed, 截至2026/10、2026Q2。

密大消費者信心

購車
• 高利環境推升民生融資壓力, 信用卡、車貸及房貸融資成本增加, 恐使拖欠率延續持續走升。此外, 亦影響耐久財

消費動能: 1)30年期房貸固定利率突破7.4%, 導致購屋與換屋門檻提高; 根據房地產經紀公司Redfin, 美國9月成屋
待售簽約量月減4%, 創2023年以來最大單月降幅, 待售房屋供給則升至2020年以來高點。2)油價走高亦增添購車
負擔, 壓縮相關消費意願。
• 本輪通膨源自能源供給、供應鏈重組等因素, 因此升息雖有助抑制需求, 但也將加重消費成本。未來若高利環境持
續影響家計單位消費, 將使本已分化的需求結構進一步惡化 , 加劇美國K型消費與資產表現分化 。即便Fed維持緊
縮立場, 但除了在控制通膨風險以外, 亦需兼顧是否抑制經濟成長、需求降溫過快的風險。

### Page 11

Cathay’s View
賭盤顯示民主黨贏得兩院選舉可能較大 政策情境分析: 若民主黨兩院勝選
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。 資料來源: Morgan Stanley, 國泰世華銀行整理。
• 美國期中選舉將於11/3舉行, 改選參眾議院與州長。由於油價大漲、通膨仍高, 共和黨與川普的民眾支持度持續下
降, 賭盤預期民主黨全面橫掃兩院的可能性上升, 扭轉目前由共和黨掌握兩院的局面。
• 若民主黨贏得兩院, 將提高國會制衡力道, 美伊衝突在選後全面開戰的可能下降; 其次, 財政政策將從減稅轉向補貼,
惟財政赤字是否改善仍須觀察; 值得留意的是, 若民主黨取得兩院與多數州長勝選, 可能提高資料中心反對建設聲
浪, 影響投資人信心。
• 然而, 並非所有共和黨都鼓勵資料中心。握有全美25%算力的德州州長Abbott有望連任, 雖是共和黨, 但政策已轉
向提高資料中心建設限制, 顯示當地資源緊迫性提升。
期中選舉｜民主黨橫掃兩院可能上升, 可能降低美伊再次開戰、但提高資料中心反彈聲浪

政策/市場 影響
關稅、移民政策 屬於總統行政權, 不受影響
美伊衝突 選後全面開戰可能下降
財政赤字 赤字持平或小幅擴張
資料中心建設 反對資料中心建設聲浪可能提高
股市 資料中心建設進度若延後將造成科技股震盪, 但基本面不受政策影響
債市 通膨、Fed政策立場影響較大20

EPS創新高, 毛利率保持在65~67%高檔 2027年預估資本支出上看850億美元
資料來源: Bloomberg預估、台積電預估。預估期間為2026Q4。 資料來源: Bloomberg預估。預估期間為2026-2027年。
• 2026年Q3 EPS 29.43元, 年增68.8%, 營收年增41.3%, 毛利率66%, 營收獲利皆優於預期與歷史新高, 毛利率符合
前次財測。展望第四季, 公司預估Q4營收523-543億美元, 營收中位數季增14%, 預估Q4毛利率65-67%, 持平並符
合預期, 但上調預估營利率至56.5-58.5%。此外, 台積電也將全年美元營收預估年增率從約40%, 升至44%。
• 2026全年預估資本支出維持不變, 保持600-640億美元。台積電未透露明年資本支出或擴廠具體計畫, 表示正在討

論德州廠規劃中。台積電重申, 2026-2028年的資本支出, 將比過去三年「更加顯著提高」。
• 預估台積電Q4 EPS可達31元以上, 全年EPS挑戰110元以上, 年增69%。

航運運費 油價走勢
原油｜地緣紛擾消息不斷, 油價高位震盪
• 面對11月即將到來的期中選舉壓力, 美國總統川普在燃料價格飆漲的逼迫下, 打出「聯俄壓油」牌, 不僅放寬制裁
向俄羅斯採購柴油, 更單方面高調宣布俄烏「能源停火」。然而, 克里姆林宮隨即轟炸烏克蘭能源設施, 戳破政治
秀的期待, 解決不了能源供應的近火。此外, 沙烏地基礎設施傳遭胡塞武裝組織轟炸, 更令市場擔憂。
• 雖然在期中選舉前, 川普雖可能持續透過政治干預、大釋戰備儲油來壓制油價, 但目前市場對這些政治動作似乎逐
漸鈍化, 加上航運運費持續攀高, 更不利能源價格放緩。
• 後市, 即便沙國以船對船等方式轉圜, 但地緣熱度難降、運費高踞, 期中選舉前政治壓力與原油供應的風險仍存, 油
價應仍維持高檔震盪格局。

營收獲利創新高, 毛利率走高 高毛利的服務營收大增為獲利動能關鍵
註: 預估範圍為2026Q4-2027Q1。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/14。
註: 預估範圍為2026Q4-2027Q1。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/14。
• ASML第三季營收116.6億歐元, 年增55%, 高於財測高標, 淨利49.6億歐元, 年增70%, 雙雙創下歷史新高。值得注
意的是, 毛利率57.45%高於前季財測的56%, 主要是高利潤的基礎安裝管理(Installed Base Management)銷售優
於預期, 反映晶片與記憶體製造商對既有產線的擴產需求強勁, 明年毛利率有望透過漲價而提升。
• 公司管理層表示, 部分訂單能見度已達2029-2030年, 且原定的2028年擴產30%已無法滿足客戶需求, 將上調產能。
過去ASML因客戶集中度高, 缺少議價能力, 但隨先進製程與封裝晶片需求大增, 客戶數量與需求成長, 並且對既有

產線更新的需求提高, 將帶動ASML後續營收與獲利成長。

通膨壓力下, 家計支出呈現負成長 實質薪資與殖利率同步走高
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/8。 資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/9/30。
• 日本8月不含食物、能源的CPI年增1.9%, 但已公布的9月東京核心CPI(不含食物、能源)年增率由8月2%跳升至3%,
暗示日本9月CPI也呈上升趨勢, 這代表日本政府雖對油價進行大規模補貼 , 但民眾所承受的通膨壓力仍舊在上升 ,
家計支出也呈現連9個月負成長。
• 另一方面, 春鬥連三年漲薪5%以上, 實質薪資年增率終於在今年轉為正值, 民眾消費能力改善。隱含企業獲利好轉,
若搭配上日本10年期公債殖利率緩步走揚, 代表經濟呈現緩步復甦態勢。惟資金環境轉趨緊縮, 加上勞動力成本上
升, 除了AI相關企業獲利仍維持強勁成長動能外, 其餘行業將面臨消費承壓及融資成本上升的雙重挑戰。

半導體設備股7月以來表現分歧 設備股評價回落至3年平均水準
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
註: 取東京威力科創、愛德萬測試、迪思科平均。
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/14。
• 日本半導體設備廠受惠 AI需求爆發將享有長期成長題材 。兩大科技龍頭中, 東京威力科創主要是半導體前段製程
設備商, 產品涵蓋塗佈/顯影機、蝕刻機等; 愛德萬測試則在半導體後段, 特別是先進封測領域具關鍵地位。目前供
應鏈前段設備交期約在3~6個月, 先進封測則面臨嚴重供不應求、交期一再延後情形, 股價表現也出現顯著差異。
• 日本設備廠具全球領導地位, 享有較大盤更高的本益比。過去3年, 設備股平均PE33.8倍, 範圍介於15~45倍之間,
在半導體受惠AI需求的長期擴廠商機加持下, 獲利有望持續抬升, 若本益比低於平均水準, 則是逢低布局的機會。

三星電子2027-2028獲利仍有望持續成長 三大廠將持續提高HBM產能佔比
註: 預估範圍為2026Q4-2028Q4。
資料來源: Bloomberg預估, 截至2026/10/14。 資料來源: J.P. Morgan預估, 截至2026/9/22。
• 三星電子第三季營業利潤率55%高於預期, 營業利潤107.4兆韓元則略低於預期, 主要受到韓元大幅升值影響 。此
外, 近期長鑫存儲發布首款4F²記憶體, 市場對於中國廠商擴產擔憂加劇, 使近期韓股表現弱勢。
• 然而, 產品發布到量產、良率合格仍需要很長的時間, 目前長鑫存儲整體技術仍落後三大廠2~3個世代。且高盛預
估中國記憶體需求至2028年將以50%的CAGR成長, 即便長鑫存儲積極擴產仍無法滿足中國自身需求, 市場對於中

國低價搶市佔的擔憂過大。此外, 美光先前財報會議時亦表示2027與2028的供需會較2026更加緊張, 三大廠持續
提高HBM產能佔比的趨勢不變, 記憶體廠未來兩年的獲利仍有持續成長的空間, 即便成長率放緩, 獲利維持時間會
遠較先前週期更長。

近期大部分市場參與者皆站在賣方 韓國個股槓桿ETF出現明顯資金流出
韓股｜回購買盤即將告終, 外資散戶同站賣方, 籌碼因素使韓股近期走弱
• 近期韓國股市走勢疲弱, 回檔幅度遠高於費半與台股, 除消息面的雜音外, 籌碼面也是原因之一。近期三星電子與
SK hynix的股票回購計畫即將額滿, 過去兩個月其它市場參與者皆站在賣方, 市場擔憂回購計畫結束後買盤將暫時
枯竭, 且韓國企業第三季的財報多數因匯率因素多數面臨下修, 在其他利空的干擾下加劇跌勢。
• 此外, 韓國個股槓桿ETF近期亦出現大量資金淨流出, 散戶在個股與槓桿ETF同步拋售, 顯示市場情緒偏向悲觀, 使
韓股評價已回落至歷史低值。然而, 即便未來兩年的獲利成長率將逐漸回落, 但在AI資本支出未歇的前提下, 韓國

企業的能夠維持在相對高位更久的時間(遠長於先前的循環), 現在進場買的是獲利的持久性而非成長率。但在股價
大幅修正後, 目前位階已具備較佳風險報酬比。

西班牙選舉民調 MSCI Spain vs Europe 權重拆解
資料來源: Bloomberg, 截至2026/10/5。 資料來源: MS, 截至2026/9/30。
歐洲｜西班牙選情利多金融股, 罷工風險成低接機會
• 民調顯示右派聯盟(PP、Vox)有望取得執政優勢(11/29大選), 市場預期新政府將取消銀行暴利稅、放寬金融監管並
重啟產業整併, 有利提升銀行獲利與資本運用效率。考量西班牙金融股占MSCI Spain權重高達45.5%, 遠高於歐洲
整體的26.9%, 在政策預期帶動下, 短期西班牙金融股乃至歐洲金融類股投資氣氛偏正向。
• 不過, 11月11日全國大罷工仍是重要觀察風險。近期民生壓力及勞資對立情緒升溫, 若抗議行動擴大, 可能影響之
後新政府改革推動速度。短期市場可望持續反映選舉紅利, 倘若罷工事件對金融股評價與歐股情緒的造成干擾, 加
上歐元區未來升息有利NIM, 反而是合適的切入點。

### Page 24

Cathay’s View
• 此前, 法國2027年財政預算案引發國會對峙, 法國CDS仍位於10月初以來的80點高位, 反映出市場對主權評級調降
與財政不確定性的政治風險溢價。然而, 這場債市風暴並非毫無防線。極右翼領袖馬琳·勒龐近期提出在2030年前
將赤字降至3%以下的影子財政計劃, 顯示即使政權可能輪替, 這為市場注入了關鍵的穩定劑 。預計信評機構下週
動作有限, 歐元短期應能止貶。
• 對歐元更核心的支撐 , 來自總體經濟的利差結構 。當前歐元區與美國的貨幣政策路徑並未出現實質性分歧 。隨著
兩大央行同步進入利率平台期或政策調整期 , 歐美升息與降息預期依然保持高度一致 。在雙方利差難以進一步擴
大的基本面大環境下, 歐元兌美元不具備單邊長期下跌的條件。
法國政治事件Timeline
資料來源: 媒體報導, 企業版ChatGPT、國泰世華整理, 截至2026/10/15 。

2026 年 10 月 15 日 全9 頁

Indicators Update 
2026 年 8 月機械受注 
製造業・非製造業（船電除く）のいずれも2 カ月ぶりに増加

経済調査部 エコノミスト 吉井 希祐

[要約] 
◼ 2026 年8 月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比＋5.2％と2 カ月ぶりに増加し、コン
センサス（Bloomberg 調査：同＋2.3％）を上回った。民需（船電除く）を 3 カ月移動
平均で見ると同＋3.6％となり、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみ
られる」に据え置いた。 
◼ 製造業からの受注額は前月比＋1.6％と 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。非鉄金属やはん用・生
産用機械、その他輸送用機械などの業種からの受注が増加した。 非製造業（船電除く）
からの受注額は同＋2.9％と 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。リース業や卸売業・小売業、通信
業などの業種からの受注が増加した。 
◼ 先行きの民需（船電除く）は 緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。企業の設備投資意
欲は強く、人手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI 関連投資などが引き続き期待され
よう。ただし、中東情勢のさらなる悪化に伴い 、受注が下振れするリスクには注意が
必要だ。事業環境の不確実性の高まりや原材料の価格高騰・供給不足に対する懸念か
ら、企業の設備投資への姿勢が慎重化する恐れがある。

図表 1：機械受注の概況（季節調整済み前 月比、％）

（注）コンセンサスは Bloomberg。

【総括】船電除く民需は 2 カ月ぶりに増加 
2026 年 8 月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比＋5.2％と 2 カ月ぶりに増加し、コンセン
サス（Bloomberg 調査：同＋2.3％）を上回った（図表2）。製造業は2 カ月ぶりに増加し、非製
造業（船電除く）も2 カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 3）。民需（船電除く）を3 カ月移動平均で見
ると同＋3.6％となり、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみられる」に据え
置いた。

【製造業】非鉄金属などが全体を押し上げたが、業種によりまちまちの結果 
2026 年 8 月の製造業からの受注額は前月比＋1.6％と 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。機種別に見る
と、原動機・重電機、輸送機械、電子・通信機械、工作機械が増加した一方、産業機械は減少
した（図表 4 左、大和総研による季節調整値） 。業種別では、17 業種中 8 業種が増加した。非
鉄金属（同＋132.6％）やはん用・生産用機械（同＋9.3％）、その他輸送用機械（同＋43.7％）
などの業種からの受注が増加した。他方、造船業（同▲ 41.0％）や電気機械（同▲5.9％）な
どからの受注は減少した。化学工業（同▲11.5％）では大型案件が 1 件あったものの、受注増
には至らなかった。

【非製造業】リース業や卸売業・小売業などを中心に増加 
2026 年8 月の非製造業（船電除く）からの受注額は前月比＋2.9％と2 カ月ぶりに増加した。
機種別に見ると、電子・通信機械、原動機・重電機が増加した一方、輸送機械、産業機械、工
作機械は減少した（図表 4 右、大和総研による季節調整値） 。業種別では、11 業種中 7 業種が
増加した。リース業（同＋136.0％）や卸売業・小売業（同＋29.2％）、通信業（同＋16.9％）
などの業種からの受注が増加した。他方、その他非製造業（同▲ 28.0％）や運輸業・郵便業
（同▲13.9％）などからの受注は減少した。

図表 4：業種別・機種別に見た機械受注額の動 き

除外したが、26 年8 月は前月比▲9.5％であった。

【外需】2 カ月連続で減少したものの依然高水準 
2026 年 8 月の外需は前月比▲7.7％と 2 カ月連続で減少した（図表 5） 。機種別では、原動
機・重電機、産業機械、電子・通信機械が減少した一方、工作機械、輸送機械は増加した（ 図
表 6、大和総研による季節調整値） 。外需は 2025 年中旬以降急増していたため、依然高水準で
あるが、増加をけん引してきた電子・通信機械の増勢にはこのところ一服感が見られる。輸送
機械で3 件、電子・通信機械で2 件、原動機・重電機で1 件の合計6 件の大型案件があった。 
機械受注の外需動向を地域別に見る上で参考になる工作機械受注を確認すると、8 月の外需
は前月比＋8.7％と3 カ月連続で増加した（日本工作機械工業会、大和総研による季節調整値、
図表 7） 。米国（同＋15.4％）からの受注は 3 カ月連続で増加した。航空・宇宙関連の大型案件
があったほか、AI 関連の受注が好調だったようだ1。欧州（EU＋英国、同＋16.9％）からの受
注も3 カ月連続で増加した。中国（同＋2.0％）からの受注は2 カ月ぶりに増加した（図表8）。 
工作機械受注は9 月分がすでに公表されており、内需は前月比▲3.3％と4 カ月ぶりに減少し
た。他方、外需は同＋2.8％と4 カ月連続で増加し、過去最高を更新し続けている。 
図表 5：一般機械の輸出と機械受注の外 需

【先行き】民需（船電除く）の緩やかな増加基調を見込むも中東情勢が下振れリスク 
先行きの民需（船電除く）は、緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。日銀短観の 9 月調査に
おける「生産・営業用設備判断 DI」 （先行き、全規模）を見ると、製造業（▲6％pt）と非製造
業（▲7％pt）のいずれでも、設備の不足感が示されている（図表 9） 。また、2026 年度の設備
投資計画（全規模、含む土地、ソフトウェアと研究開発投資額は含まない）は、製造業（前年
度比＋8.3％） 、非製造業（同＋7.3％）ともに堅調に推移している（図表 10）。企業の設備投資
意欲は強く、人手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI 関連投資などが引き続き期待されよう。 
ただし、中東情勢のさらなる悪化に伴い、受注が下振れするリスクには注意が必要だ。ホル
ムズ海峡の通航が困難な状況が続く中、サウジアラビアとイエメンの親イラン武装組織フーシ
の間で攻撃の応酬が続くなど、エネルギー供給をめぐる先行き不透明感は強い。事業環境の不
確実性の高まりや原材料の価格高騰・供給不足に対する懸念から、企業の 設備投資への姿勢が
慎重化する恐れがある。 
図表 9：機械受注額と生産・営業用設備判断 DI（全規模）

（注 1）機械受注額は季節調整値。太線は 3 カ月移動平均。 
（注 2）生産・営業用設備判断 DI の直近値は先行き、それ以外は最近。 
（出所）内閣府、日本銀行統計より大和総研作成

股價相對大盤走勢

華泰 (2329 TT)  Trading Buy

獲利逐步增加，建議 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ：華泰目前 IC 封測業務佔營收 58%，受惠 NAND Flash 
轉為賣方市場與 AI 儲存需求，封測稼動率回升至約 70%；EMS
業務佔 42%， 主要由佔其營收逾 70% 的美系 AI 伺服器核心客戶
拉貨所帶動。隨全球資料中心算力升級， AI 伺服器出貨量維持
增長， 2Q27 新建廠房正式投產，華泰在 AI 伺服器主板、 eSSD 
一條龍封測與模組代工份額將進一步擴大，推升 2H26 至 2027
全年之營運動能與獲利能見度。預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分
別為 2.33 元和 3.17 元。華泰因美系大客戶公司的治理與法規合
規爭議大，再加上記憶體封測的評價較低，因此華泰的整體本
益比低於同業。但華泰隨著 2H26 ASP 提升和新產品貢獻，獲利
逐步增加，因此投資為 Trading Buy ，目標價 50.6 元。  
2Q26 稅後 EPS 0.63 元：記憶體客戶備貨積極，半導體營收回
升，佔全公司比重上升，使產能利用率維持高檔。 EMS 事業中
心則受惠 AI 伺服器客戶需求佳。華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元，
QoQ+12.67% ，其中半導體事業部 2Q26 營收達 31.67 億元，
QoQ+16.26% ， 半導體事業產品營收比重分別為 ： 記憶體佔 72%、
邏輯 IC 佔 16% 及測試服務佔 12% 。由於邏輯 IC 有部份是 SSD 
controller ，佔全公司營收比重增加至 58% 。 EMS 部份，營收為  
23.09 億元， QoQ+8.10% ，佔全公司營收比重為 42% 。 2Q26 毛
利率來自半導體事業產能利用率維持高檔 。 2Q26 毛利率 13.25%
較 1Q26 增加 0.46 個百分點， 2Q26 稅後 EPS 0.62 元。  
預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元： 華泰過去兩年的年度
資本支出維持在 20 億元左右， 1H26 資本支出已達約 14.77 億
元，加計新建廠房與設備投入，預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過
25 億元。為突破現有廠房產能擁擠瓶頸並因應高階封測需求激
增，華泰於高雄園區建置之全新 9 層樓高先進封裝廠房正全面
進行無塵室機電工程，預計於 2Q27 正式落成並投產。公司採取
審慎務實的階梯式擴充策略，第一階段將優先啟用其中 3 個樓
層建置高等級無塵室，

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 5 日

2 群益投顧 
  華泰 ESG ：  
華泰一直以來所秉持的企業核心價值為「誠正信實、主動創新、異體同心、
積極用心」 ，落實企業誠信及企業道德標準是企業永續治理的關鍵，透過有
效的公司治理架構與董事會職能強化公司治理之作為以不斷創造企業的價
值；除外，公司設有薪酬委員會以保障員工福利並維繫公司競爭力，營運過
程以內控制度確保相關程序之正確性也是營運透明化很重要的基礎。  
環境方面，華泰電子在營運上推行許多節省能資源、減少廢棄物以及妥當處
理廢污水、廢棄物的措施。未來也將持續關注氣候變遷與全球暖化議題，對
於溫室氣體排放進行系統化管理與統計，共同維護自然環境、為地球永續盡
一份心力。  
華泰電子極力追尋為員工創造優質職場環境以及專業能力的培訓。不僅是
人才的培育、員工的身心狀態的健康，期望能創造出幸福的健康職場，展現
華泰人的活力。  
華泰主要業務為 IC 封裝與測試製造服務、與專業電子代工製造服務：  
華泰成立於 06/1971 ，為電子產品製造服務和積體電路封裝測試製造服務供
應商，擁有成品事業中心和半導體事業中心兩大事業部門。半導體事業以
NAND Flash 封裝測試為主；成品事業部則為 EMS 代工，以伺服器為主，
SSD 在近年也成為主力之一，全公司目前 5,800 人。頎邦 (6147 TT) 為最大股
東，持股 35.56% 。  
華泰電子製造服務 (EMS/CEM) 服務項目涵蓋：印刷電路板佈線 (PCB 
Layout) 、製造最佳化設計 (DFM) 、測試最佳化設計 (DFT) 、快速原型組裝
(Prototype) 、印刷電路板組裝量產 (PCB Assembly) 、功能性 /可靠度測試
(Function &Reliability Testing) 、系統組裝 (System integration) 等。積體電路
(IC) 封裝測試服務涵蓋：積體電路 (IC) 及各種半導體零組件封裝測試。公司
自 2003 年開始進行產品線及市場定位調整，已將半導體營運重心聚焦於
NAND Flash 各項應用產品之封裝測試。並引進策略性股東金士頓，也是華
泰 NAND Flash 的主要客戶 ； 而EMS 的主要客戶則為 Supermicro(SMCI US) ，
其佔 EMS 約 70% 營收。  
華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元， QoQ+12.67% ，稅後淨利 4.08 億元，
QoQ+73.97% ，稅後 EPS 0.62 元：  
半導體事業中心受惠記憶體產業成為賣方市場，記憶體客戶備貨積極，半導
體營收回升，佔全公司比重上升，使產能利用率維持高檔。 EMS 事業中心
則受惠 AI 伺服器客戶需求佳，再加上記憶體客戶備貨積極，一條龍生產企
業級  SSD 模組，使 2Q26 營運持續向上。  
華泰 2Q26 營收 54.76 億元 ，QoQ+12.67% ， 其中半導體事業部因 NAND Flash
客戶訂單增加明顯， NAND Flash 價格持續上升，華泰的模組客戶積極擴大
下單。半導體事業部營運展現成長趨勢， 2Q26 營收達 31.67 億元，
QoQ+16.26% ，半導體事業產品營收比重分別為：記憶體佔 72%、邏輯 IC 佔
16% 及測試服務佔 12%。由於邏輯 IC 有部份是 SSD controller ，因此半導體
的封測是以記憶體為主 。 2Q26 半導體事業佔全公司營收比重由 1Q26 的 56% 
增加至 58% 。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 5 日

3 群益投顧 
2Q26 EMS 部份，主要營收來源以伺服器、工業電腦與消費性電子為主，其
中伺服器相關產品受惠  AI 伺服器與資料中心算力建設強勁拉貨，美系核
心 AI 伺服器客戶訂單佔整體 EMS 營收比重超過 70% ，承製  GPU/CPU 主
機板與周邊加速板 (SMT/PCBA) 維持高速出貨，帶動 EMS 業績穩健攀升。
2Q26 EMS 營收為  23.09 億元， QoQ+8.10% ，佔全公司營收比重為 42% 。  
2Q26 毛利率增加 ， 來自半導體事業產能利用率維持高檔 ， 產能利用率約 70%
和 1Q26 相近，因為產能在持續擴充中，產能利用率提升較慢。 2Q26 毛利
率 13.25% 較 1Q26 增加 0.46 個百分點，但較 2Q25 減少 1.72 個百分點，最
主要是折舊費用增加，和產品組合改變， single die 封測佔比提升 (消費性電
子降低容量，堆疊的量較低 )， 2Q26 稅後淨利 4.08 億元， QoQ+73.97% ，稅
後 EPS 0.62 元。  
營運資金效率同步提升，負債比率由 1Q26 的 49.3% 大幅降至 38.44% ，應
收帳款週轉天數自 1Q26 的 87 天降至 74 天 ， 存貨週轉天數亦由1Q26 的  59 
天改善至 48 天。  
2Q26 股東權利大幅增加，部份來自子公司持有頎邦約 30,320 張，約佔頎邦
持股 4%，1H26 認列於其他綜合損益之金融資產未實現評價利益 (FVOCI) 約
增加 50.5 億元，惟考量該部份為未實現評價利益，且頎邦股價後續仍可波
動，因此 2026 年底股東權益採較保守假設。  
預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元：  
華泰過去兩年的年度資本支出維持在 20 億元左右， 2024 年約 19.59 億元、
2025 年達 23.16 億元。 1H26 資本支出已達約 14.77 億元，加計新建廠房與
設備投入，預估 2026 全年資本支出將超過 25 億元。資本支出重點包含高度
自動化晶圓薄化、多晶粒垂直堆疊等高階高容量記憶體封裝設備。建置新廠
之覆晶封裝 (Flip Chip) 與系統級封裝 (SiP) 專用生產線。 2027 年資本支出預
算額度，將待年底董事會審議後定案。  
產能擴充時程與硬體資本支出規劃方面，為突破現有廠房產能擁擠瓶頸並
因應高階封測需求激增，華泰於高雄園區建置之全新 9 層樓高先進封裝廠
房正全面進行無塵室機電工程，預計於 2Q27 正式落成並投產。該新廠總樓
地板面積約達 7 萬平方公尺，公司採取審慎務實的階梯式擴充策略，第一階
段將優先啟用其中 3 個樓層建置高等級無塵室，主力配置覆晶封裝 (Flip 
Chip) 及系統級封裝 (SiP) 全自動化產線，並承接自企業總部騰挪出之覆晶產
能，騰出之總部空間則可彈性擴充傳統打線封裝機台 (Wire Bonder) ，以承接
一線封測大廠產能挪移至先進封裝所釋出之成熟邏輯  IC 外溢訂單。  
2Q26 毛利率較 2Q25 同期略為承壓，主因之一即為設備與新廠投資所衍生。
2024 年約 8.34 億元、 2025 年達 9.75 億元。 1H26 約 5.55 億元。 9 層樓新廠
採取分樓層逐步啟用的模式，正是為避免一次性提列過高折舊成本，以維護
資本支出與折舊費用的健康平衡。  
預估華泰 2026 年營收 215.74 億元， YoY+9.61% ，稅後獲利 15.34 億元，
YoY+8.77% ，稅後 EPS 2.33 元：  
先前華泰半導體封測與 EMS 代工業務營收比重約各為 60% ～ 70% 與 30% ～
40%，整體獲利動能與半導體封測景氣循環緊密連動。以往封測代工代價談

### Page 4

個股報告

4 群益投顧 
判為後段產業常態，平均銷售單價 (ASP) 具競爭壓力，惟隨 AI 帶動封測需求
增加，大廠訂單外溢，高階儲存需求激增與原廠比重持續拉升，機台稼動率
提升對毛利率之正向營運槓桿效益顯著；加上公司已於 2H26 針對部分緊俏
封測產能微幅調漲價格，有助獲利結構持續優化。  
華泰半導體事業中心因應邊緣裝置與高階資料傳輸對巨量頻寬與極致縮裝
的需求，全面推進次世代封裝技術架構升級。公司目前主力發展之複合鍵合
/混合封裝， Hybrid Bonding ，整合打線 Wire Bond 與覆晶 Flip Chip 之多晶
粒 SiP 系統級封裝技術 )，已成功量產並出貨應用於  micro SD 7.0 、eUFS 4.x
及 uSSD 等高階記憶體產品，將記憶體晶粒 (Memory Die) 透過打線堆疊、主
控晶片 (Controller) 透過覆晶技術共同整合封裝於單一模組內 ， 作為未來高附
加價值產品線之核心成長動能。在次世代先進封裝中長期研發方面，華泰亦
深化與核心客戶合作，積極開發扇出型覆晶晶圓級封裝 (Fan-Out FCCSP) 等
高階架構，預計於 4Q26 至 2027 年逐步推進至工程試產階段，卡位高速邊
緣運算與客製化 ASIC 儲存市場。  
目前已有 2 家原廠進入華泰前十大客戶名單，並陸續有新原廠接洽驗證。原
廠級訂單具備量體大、能見度長之特性。低容量 eMMC EOL 紅利，受原廠
產能轉移至高階  AI 儲存影響，成熟低容量 eMMC 陸續進入停產 (EOL) 階
段，缺口由台灣及區域晶片商填補，帶動國內記憶體客戶下單積極。  
EMS 事業中心則維持高速成長動能，伺服器主機板 (Server PCBA) 與核心客
戶維持長年深度合作，伺服器相關訂單佔 EMS 營收比重達 70% ，其中美系
AI 伺服器核心大客戶 Supermicro 佔整體伺服器營收比重逾 90% ；由於
Supermicro 訂單模式以帶料生產為主 ， 其營收規模與獲利貢獻顯著高於未帶
料之純加工模組。隨全球資料中心算力升級引領  AI 伺服器出貨量維持增
長，2Q27 新建廠房正式投產，華泰在  AI 伺服器主板、 eSSD 一條龍封測與
模組代工份額將進一步擴大，推升 2H26 至 2027 全年之營運動能與獲利能
見度。預估 2026 年和 2027 年 EPS 分別為 2.33 元和 3.17 元。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

King Slide

Maintain Buy: Sep sales -31% MoM from 
high bases; Aug earnings remain solid

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 14,888 TWD | Price: 13,100 TWD

Sep sales -31% MoM from high bases; 3Q26 sales in-line 
King Slide’s Sep sales of NT$4.46bn declined 31% MoM (+196% YoY), after the strong 
sales in the past few months (up 17-46% MoM in Apr-Jul and up 1% MoM in Aug). 3Q26 
sales of NT$17.34bn (+60% QoQ, +297% YoY) were 0.5% above BofAe of NT$17.26bn 
and 0.9% below consensus of NT$17.49bn.  
We are not surprised with the MoM sales drop in Sep as (1) the company’s MoM sales 
trend has been volatile (Exhibit 1 and Exhibit 2), (2) sales bases in Apr-Aug were at 
record levels, (3) contribution from Nvidia Rubin GPU projects should be more 
meaningful in 4Q26 after hardware components ramp-up, and (4) partial general server 
orders/demand likely were pushed out due to key component shortage (e.g. memory, 
CPU). 
Solid Aug EPS NT$40.07; pre-tax/net margin 74.6%/59.1% 
In addition to Sep sales, on the same day (6 Oct), King Slide also announced its Aug 
unaudited key financial data as required by local regulations (Exhibit 3). Aug sales were 
NT$6,466mn (+344% YoY), while pre-tax profit was NT$4,826mn (+216% YoY) and net 
income was NT$3,819mn (+212% YoY). EPS came in at NT$40.07 (+212% YoY). Pre-tax 
margin and net margin were 74.6% and 59.1%, respectively.  
Compared to July unaudited figures announced on 14 Aug (see our 17 Aug report), 
including sales of NT$6,407mn, pre-tax margin of 93.4%, net margin of 74.7%, and EPS 
of NT$50.24, we believe the margin and EPS differences mainly reflected FX impact 
under similar sales scale. 
Combining Jul and Aug unaudited results, sales totaled NT$12.87bn and EPS reached 
NT$90.31. Pre-tax margin and net margin were 84.0% and 66.9%, respectively. Jul + Aug 
sales achieved 75%/74% of BofAe/consensus 3Q26 sales. Pre-tax profit, net income, 
and EPS all achieved 79% of BofAe (79.4% pre-tax margin, 62.9% net margin, and 
NT$113.87 EPS) and 77-78% of consensus (80.3% pre-tax margin, 63.1% net margin, 
and NT$117.62 EPS). We also noted that Jul + Aug EPS of NT$90.31 already surpassed 
EPS of NT$74.38 in 2Q26 with similar pre-tax/net margin (84.0%/66.9% in Jul + Aug, vs. 
84.2%/65.4% in 2Q26). 
Maintain Buy; expect a neutral-to-positive market reaction 
We expect a neutral-to-positive market reaction in the near term in view of significant 
MoM sales decline in Sep but also still solid Aug unaudited data. We maintain Buy on 
King Slide as the server rail market leader with broad and diversified client/project base. 
We expect the company to post 3Q26 results after market close on 12 Nov (Thu).

Price objective basis & risk 
King Slide (KGSLF) 
Our PO of NT$14,888 is based on 32x 2027E EPS. We use 32x P/E multiple (2.5SD 
above P/E average since listing) in view of strong AI server market demand, leading 
position in server rail, solid/high margin profiles, and double-digit sales/EPS CAGR in 
2026-28E. Its early step into server market since 2001 with proven track record, much 
larger scale than peer, and 3,600+ patents as strong moats for high/above-peer margins. 
Its above-30x P/E is a similar case to other component sub-sector leaders in Taiwan.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) even stronger AI server demand, (2) margin upside thanks 
to larger scale and/or better efficiency, (3) better kitchen slides market demand, (4) 
faster capacity expansion, (5) favorable FX movement (mainly USD/NTD) for GM and/or 
non-operating items.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) softer AI server demand due to GPU/components 
shortage, (2) GM pressure due to higher raw material cost (e.g. steel, aluminum), (3) 
softer kitchen slides market demand under macro uncertainty/weakness, (4) delayed 
product certification, (5) slower capacity expansion, (6) unfavorable FX movement 
(mainly USD/NTD) to weigh on GM and/or non-operating items.

Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Hermes Testing Solutions
Shifting-left, scaling-up: dual catalysts in chip
probing and CPO
Jack-of-all-trades for bespoke testing solutions in the semiconductor industry
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 PT. HTSI is
uniquely positioned to benefit from two key semiconductor testing trends: shift-left
testing and the emerging CPO testing market. Leveraging its long-standing relationship

with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across electrical test engineering,
customised test equipment, thermal control modules, probe cards, and CPO testing
solutions. We forecast revenue growth of 127%/152%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, driven
by increasing adoption of thermal control modules for wafer-level test and initial
deployment of CPO insertion 2 and insertion 3 test equipment through ficonTEC.
Additional 2028E growth drivers include reflow tools, laser clean solutions, and MEMS

probe cards. The stock trades at 27/15x 2027/28E PE, versus ~35x NTM PE for handler-
equipment peers, offering re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified,
and its CPO presence expands. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Shift-left beneficiary with exceptional thermal control modules
Thermal control modules should be HTSI's largest growth driver over the next several
years. We expect increasing chip power consumption, rising package complexity, and

tighter probe pitches to drive adoption of advanced thermal control solutions used
alongside leading-edge probers, in additional to more wafer level insertions and longer
test time. We estimate HTSI will ship approximately 3,000 thermal-control modules in
2027E, up from around 1,200 units in 2026E, sales contribution rise from ~30% in
Q226 to >50% of sales by 2027E. The launch of its second-generation platform in
2027E, which carries 25-50% higher ASPs, should further improve profitability. We

forecast GM expansion from 55.5% in 2026E to 58.3%/60.0% in 2027E/28E.
Unique position to capture CPO upside
Through its partnership with German optical-automation leader ficonTEC, HTSI is
positioned to participate in the initial ramp of CPO testing equipment. HTSI supports
local manufacturing, installation, automation and after-sales service, making it a key
enabler of volume production. We estimate HTSI captures approximately 30% of
equipment value across the 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems we forecast for

delivery in 2027E, with further potential upside in 2028E as adoption expands.
Valuation: initiate coverage with a Buy rating and price target NT$8,880
We forecast EPS of NT$29.1 in 2026E (including a one-off employee share purchase
programme impact), rising to NT$153.3/286.5 in 2027E/28E. Our NT$8,880 price target
is based on 31x average 2028E PE, versus handler-equipment peers forward PE at
roughly 35x, reflecting HTSI's unique exposure to both shift-left testing, CPO, and our

estimate of 32% LT earnings CAGR. We initiate with a Buy rating.

Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will HTSI be able to keep its moat within chip probing thermal control modules?
Yes. We expect HTSI's competitive position to strengthen as chip power consumption, package
complexity, and probe density continue to increase. The company is developing next-generation
thermal control solutions, including liquid cooling technologies, while also expanding into specialized

chuck solutions designed to address wafer warpage. In addition, HTSI's close collaboration with
TSMC, TEL, Advantest, and other OSATs should reinforce its position within advanced wafer-level
testing.
Q: Will insertion 2 of CPO testing process be skipped?
No. We believe concerns surrounding insertion 2 adoption are overdone. While some industry
participants favor bypassing insertion 2 due to throughput and ROI considerations, doing so increases
the risk of defective optical engines being identified only after the components are delivered to

OSATs, creating yield accountability challenges and higher packaging losses. Based on our industry
studies, we expect insertion 2 testing to remain an important part of the CPO workflow over at least
the next two years as the industry focuses on improving PIC yields and overall testing efficiency.
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 price target. HTSI
offers unique exposure to two of the semiconductor testing industry's most attractive growth drivers:

1) shift-left testing and 2) CPO testing. The industry's shift-left testing strategy will have more test
insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in addition to longer CP time. Leveraging its
longstanding relationship with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across thermal control
modules, electrical-test engineering services, customized equipment, and emerging CPO testing
solutions. We believe there could be re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified, and

its CPO presence expands.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI currently trades at just 15x of our 2028E EPS versus

handler-equipment peers forward PE at roughly 35x. We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO
testing equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on
our industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E,
this should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands. Our

reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR
vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for
re-rating.

Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
Figure 1: Semiconductor testing process: from wafer level to package level

Source: UBS
We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector will standout among competition, with
greater sales CAGR comparing to the industry.
We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now got substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damages.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years, bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.

 Thermal control module ASP expansion.  Gen 2 thermal control modules will
start to ship in 2027E which will drive tool ASP up around 25-50%.
 Demand supported by Advantest expansions. We believe the 3,000 thermal
control modules shipment forecast for 2027E is not far-fetched, as we read-across
from Advantest's announced capacity expansion plan (testers will be paired with
probers with a 1:1 ratio) with increasing capacity up to 5,000 testers within

FY2027/3 and they also announced a 10,000-unit target within CY2028.
Although the testers capacity expansion plan includes testers for final test, we do
see a good portion to be allocated to chip probing given shift-left testing trend
increasing both testing insertions and testing time before chip packaging.

and 3 testing equipment demand starts to rise in 2027 with scale-out CPO

adoption, we see HTSI to ship 40/70 insertion 2 and 3 equipment in 2027E, with

proliferates, and driving 52% 2026-31E test equipment & engineering services

 Insertion 2 might scale down, but not happening anytime soon. Insertion 2
equipment shipment might come down in 2029E and beyond, as foundries scale-

up yield for photonic IC (PIC) in order to lessen the time consuming insertion 2
testing burden.

Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe HTSI is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers and
capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see HTSI is uniquely positioned as
the main beneficiary of two of the strongest growth drivers within semiconductor
testing: testing shift-left and CPO.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing

### Page 5

The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging.
Figure 4: CP is set to grow substantially with shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step

RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
CP (Die B)
Assembly Test (AST)
oS
Source: UBS
The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while
defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed

testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million
(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall

integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of
wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level

chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

Hermes Testing (HTSI) is a key beneficiary of this trend. Leveraging its long-standing
relationship with TSMC and expertise in semiconductor test solutions, HTSI supplies

advanced thermal control modules used alongside leading-edge probers (made by
Tokyo Electron), particularly for AI applications. These thermal control modules are
required for high TDP semiconductor wafer probe in order to maintain the temperature

### Page 6

of the chuck to avoid needle burns or probe card damages. We estimate HTSI will ship
approximately 3,000 thermal-management modules in 2027E, up from 1,200 in 2026E,
supporting sales growth of 127%/152% in 2026E/27E. HTSI is also the partner for
prober installation, prober maintenance and services. In addition, HTSI provides high-
end chuck processing services, an area that should benefit from increasing package sizes
and higher wafer warpage associated with advanced packaging.

Beyond thermal control modules, HTSI also supplies consumables and customised
solutions for wafer-level testing, including probe card cleaning sheets and laser-based
probe head cleaning systems. As probing intensity increases under the shift-left testing
model, demand for cleaning and maintenance solutions should rise alongside probe
card utilisation. Currently, cleaning sheets is around 5% of sales, but we believe it could
gain traction in 2027/28E and beyond. We see additional upside from future modular

laser cleaning systems that could be integrated directly with probers, further expanding
HTSI's content opportunity within advanced wafer-level testing.
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
As interconnect bottlenecks increasingly shift from the memory wall to the copper wall,
we believe co-packaged optics (CPO) adoption is inevitable over the longer term. Testing
remains one of the key bottlenecks to CPO deployment, as optical validation differs

fundamentally from the electrical testing processes that have dominated semiconductor
manufacturing for decades. As a result, CPO is creating a new testing opportunity across
the semiconductor equipment and consumables ecosystem. We have written
extensively on CPO and opportunities that it brings to the semiconductor supply chain,
see our CPO deep dive , our thoughts on CPO timing , 2026 SiPhIA conference
takeaways, and SEMICON 2026 takeaways.
Various test equipment suppliers are scrambling to come up with ideal testing

equipment across the four insertions of CPO testing, with solutions to ensure both
optical and electrical signal integrity of the CPO solutions. The four insertions are:
 Insertion 1: Electronic IC (EIC)/Photonic IC (PIC) test.  Single-side wafer test
that has dedicated probe cards to test the fabricated EIC/PIC wafers.
 Insertion 2: Optical/Electrical test. Double sided wafer test, testing the hybrid
bonded (e.g. via TSMC's COUPE) EIC and PIC wafers by an opto-electrical test that

verifies the electrical signals on the top and optical signal on the bottom of the
hybrid bonded wafers.
 Insertion 3: Optical engine (OE) test.  The OE tests are done to ensure the
quality of the OEs and with FAU (Fiber Array Unit) attached, to ensure the signal
integrity of the OEs. The testing equipment needs to be able to handle 224G per
channel data rates, requiring high unit per hour (UPH) throughput, meticulous dirt
mitigation, and maintaining a near 100% contact rating on high-speed sockets.

 Insertion 4: ASIC final test.  This final test step is established post the OEs are
packaged together with the ASIC. The complete ASIC package (now with CPO) is
now tested for opto-electrical functionalities.
As it stands, insertion 2 and insertion 3 are the two areas where there are the most
debates in the industry. Insertion 3 suppliers such as Chroma has noted the trend of
“shift-to-middle” paradigm centered on insertion 3. In high-density scale-out switches

deploying up to 32 optical engines (e.g. Nvidia’s Spectrum-X architectures), any OE
defect results in package scrap at the CoWoS level, making 100% known good die
(KGD) validation at the singulated OE stage an absolute commercial requirement.

With its 25 years of precision test equipment expertise, ficonTEC is an early mover for
CPO test equipment and has key know-how for optical alignment, which is
quintessential for optical test. HTSI, through its partnership with ficonTEC, is set to be a
major beneficiary once CPO proliferates in 2027E-28E. As the partner of ficonTEC,
Hermes Testing is the key enabler that can help ficonTEC achieve HVM for its CPO
testing equipment. As of now, our view is that both insertion 2 and insertion 3 should be

required within the next two years, despite the difficulties surrounding double-sided
wafer probing (insertion 2), as the yield for OEs are still not high enough to avoid
defective dies. Finding out known-good OEs will be crucial before the FAU coupling
process during the latter stages of CPO packaging, to prevent impeding the final
package with multiple OEs attached to the switch ASIC (e.g. 32 OEs on Nvidia's
Spectrum-X). We estimate Hermes Testing will ship 40/70 units of insertion 2/3 testing

equipment in 2027E, with potential upside to the numbers as we view Hermes Testing
will likely assist with the ramp up for Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment in 2027E. Gen 2
insertion equipment will come with better throughput and ASP .

Beyond insertions 2 and 3, HTSI is also positioned to participate in insertion 1 testing
through customised prober solutions for photonic integrated circuit (PIC) testing. We
believe these solutions could eventually be packaged as licensable intellectual property,

creating an additional long-term growth opportunity as CPO adoption broadens. While
near-term testing growth is driven by AI accelerators, CPO represents the next major
expansion cycle for semiconductor testing. We believe HTSI is uniquely positioned to
participate across multiple test insertions, providing exposure to a potentially significant
source of incremental revenue beginning in 2027E.

HTSI's unique position for new TAM of thermal control
modules and CPO test equipment
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, as a spin-off
from Hermes-Epitek. The company provides semiconductor testing and inspection

solutions, engineering services, customized equipment, and test consumables. HTSI
maintains a global service footprint across Taiwan (Hsinchu, Tainan), Mainland China
(Suzhou), Japan (Tokyo, Kumamoto), Singapore, Malaysia, and the United States
(Arizona), enabling close support for leading semiconductor customers.
Its main business segments are structured around three pillars:
 Semiconductor equipment and customized products : HTSI utilises a dual-

track strategy of independent R&D and also equipment agency sales. They offers

### Page 9

highly customized solutions designed to address specific client pain points and
bottlenecks. Key products include: thermal control modules that pair with a 1:1
ratio with wafer-level test probers, electron beam inspection equipment, white
light interference optical inspection systems, laser probe cleaning machines, and
the SYMPHONY fluxless reflow system.
 Testing equipment engineering services:  The company operates the largest
engineering service team in Taiwan, consisting of over 340 technical professionals.

The engineering service team provides extensive equipment life-cycle support,
including installation, relocations, modification, testing, routine maintenance, and
repair services. CPO related equipment sales/services will be recognized within this
segment, as CPO are not direct HTSI products, but rather sales recognition via its
partnership with ficonTEC.
 Probe card design, manufacturing, and cleaning materials:  HTSI delivers a
comprehensive product portfolio that supports the entire probe card life-cycle. For

probe cards, they supply membrane, cantilever, and vertical probe cards. As they
are small in scale, HTSI targets niche probe card applications such as RF, 5G/6G,
millimeter wave, LCD driver, low end logics, TIA (Transimpedance Amplifier), etc. In
addition to the three existing probe technologies, HTSI also collaborates with MJC
in Japan for MEMS probe development, targeting sales in 2028E for high-end, AI/
HPC related applications. Cleaning materials is another important business within

this segment, as HTSI source and process sand paper or other materials to offer
cleaning sheets to clean the probe cards, removing oxidations, unwanted particles
and substances that accumulated on the probe cards during the testing process.
The sales resiliency in the coming years mainly stems from its robust relationship with key
industry players, especially as an engineering and service provider with TSMC for testing
solutions. This sets up a solid foundation for its customized equipment business, as they

know the specific testing bottleneck to tackle. For instance, as Hermes-Epitek
manufacturers the probers for Tokyo Electron (TEL) and HTSI helps with installation,
services, and maintenance of the probers, they understand the thermal bottlenecks that
the wafer-level test industry has been facing and can effectively come up with module
solutions to solve this.
Margins set to expand with value-added products and services
The company has demonstrated strong execution in advanced wafer-level testing, with

H126 revenue of NT$2.2bn already exceeding 90% of FY25 revenue (NT$2.4bn).
Profitability has also improved significantly, with GM increasing from 42.1% in 2025 to
54.9% in H126.
With share of value-added products and services set to rise in 2027E in sales mix, HTSI is
set to see GM and operating leverage expansion. As a niche engineering service provider
that has robust ties to leading foundries and OSATs, we believe HTSI has substantial
pricing power and estimate HTSI' GM for thermal control modules to be around 65%,

with GM for CPO equipment of around 60% as HTSI provides an engineering-service-
like business for it. We believe these factors will help the company to reach a 60% long-
term GM range.
OPM is also set to expand with customized equipment sales rise in total sales mix. If we
exclude the one-time expense to be recognized during Q326 for employee stock
benefits (we estimate around NT$1bn), YoY opex growth will be around 99% for
2027E, with downside risk if the company conducts tight cost control measures. We

forecast the company's OPM will expand from 19.5% in 2026E, to 44.3%/47.0% in
2027/28E.

Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI still trades at just 15x 2028E P/E. We
believe market expectations for 2027E CPO testing equipment shipments remain
conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our industry analysis, we
estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this should
be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3
shipment due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO

adoption expands. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM
level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for re-rating.
We believe the market is not fully reflecting three key growth drivers:
1) Shift-left testing is still underappreciated.
As advanced packaging costs rise and AI accelerators become increasingly complex,
more thorough testing is done at the wafer-level. This should drive sustained demand

for HTSI's thermal control modules, probe card consumables, and other wafer-level
testing solutions.
2) CPO revenue potential remains underestimated.
Investor expectations for 2027 CPO equipment shipments, particularly insertion 2
testing systems, remain conservative. Based on our industry studies, we forecast HTSI
will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, with further potential
upside in 2028E as CPO adoption broadens.
3) New products provide additional optionality.

Beyond its core businesses, HTSI is expanding into higher-value solutions including mass-
reflow tools, MEMS probe cards and laser cleaning systems, creating incremental
growth opportunities that we believe are largely absent from current expectations.
We value HTSI using 31x average 2028E PE, underpinned by a 32% 2027-31E earnings
CAGR. We use 2028E PE as our view is that 2028 is the year for thermal control modules
and CPO adoption grow from the initial ramping up stage to mass adoption stage. We

believe using handlers as a peer group is justifiable for a unique company like HTSI, with
thermal control modules for chip probing sharing similar characteristics as active thermal
control used for final test, in addition to Hon Precision and Chroma having exposure to
CPO test equipment. For equipment vendors, the peer group's average NTM PE post
2022 is around 35x, and the group is currently trading at around 41x NTM PE (see
What's Priced In chart).

Risk to the current share price is heavily skewed (2.2:1) to the upside.
UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected thermal
module and CPO equipment shipment in 2027-28E, with additional upside from better
traction of probe cards and mass reflow tools, forming 157%/91% YoY sales growth in
2027/28E and even higher operating leverage with OPM of 46.3%/50.0% in 2027/28E.

This scenario implies a 34x 2028E PE, a upside driving by more equipment demand
stemming for robust shift-left test induced equipment bottleneck, alongside CPO
proliferation with scale-up CPO equipment pull-in in 2028E.
BASE (NT$8,880): Our price target of NT$8,880 is based on 31x 2028E PE. We expect an

requirement during testing, alongside 40/70 CPO insertion 2/3 shipment. We see these

growth drivers driving YoY sales growth from a high base of 127% in 2026E to
152%/78% YoY in 2027/2028E, with OPM of 44.3%/47.0% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,500): Our downside scenario reflects lower shipment of thermal
control modules due to less advanced prober demand with less insertion 2 and 3
equipment shipment due to bottlenecks with CPO production, alongside limited
additional growth drivers to be picked up in 2028 such as MEMS probe card and mass

reflow tools. This leads to weaker YoY sales growth of 67%/50% in 2027/28E and
operating margin of 29.3%/32.0% in 2027/28E as equipment shipment fails to scale.
This scenario implies a 14x 2028E PE, as valuation de-rate with dimmed outlook without
CPO and limited upside from thermal control module shipments.

Company Description
Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu,
Taiwan. It focuses on providing semiconductor testing and
inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support,
and maintenance services. Its main business segments are
customized semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and
probe cards and other materials.
Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC

applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on
providing semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical
support, and maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized
semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Hermes Testing based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less
investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development

such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected front-end/CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in
Hermes Testing gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.
- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from

either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
Hermes Testing losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
Hermes Testing, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.
- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of

the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact Hermes
Testing’s margins for equipment shipments.

Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Document 105: 8月海外资金重返美债市场，英国持仓首破万亿，中日继续减持、中国持债08年来新低

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月海外资金重返美债市场，英国持仓首破万亿，中日继续减持、中国持债08年来新低.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

据美财政部报告， 8 ⽉海外投资者净买⼊美债 1089 亿美元，持债规模扭转两⽉连降；⼗⼤
持债国家地区中， 8 ⽉只有中⽇持仓下滑，头号 “ 债主 ” ⽇本在⼲预汇市当⽉持仓创 22 个⽉新
低、连降四个⽉，中国持仓连降三个⽉，⼀年来减少 1090 亿美元，第⼆⼤ “ 债主 ” 英国持仓
两⽉连创历史新⾼，加拿⼤ 8 ⽉增持居⾸、增幅 383 亿美元，总持仓排第五，增幅第⼆位⽐
利时增 198 亿美元。
摘要：
美国官⽅数据显⽰，今年 8 ⽉，海外资⾦重返美国国债市场，但主要海外持有国家地区的配置⽅
向仍然分化，前三⼤ “ 债主 ” 中，⽇本和中国⼤陆继续减持，英国持仓续创新⾼。
美国财政部于美东时间 16 ⽇周五公布国际资本流动报告（ TIC ），披露 8 ⽉海外投资者净买⼊美国
国债 1089 亿美元，扭转 7 ⽉净卖出 36 亿美元的抛售势头。按持仓余额统计， 8 ⽉海外投资者持有
的美国国债总规模环⽐增加 833 亿美元，升⾄ 9.3317 万亿美元，在两个⽉连降后反弹。
美债的主要持有国家地区中，英国持仓规模史上⾸次突破 1 万亿美元，加拿⼤、⽐利时也⼤幅增

持。但海外资⾦重返美债市场，并不意味着所有主要持有国都在增加配置。⽇本的持仓⾃ 5 ⽉以
来持续下滑，和⽇本政府抛售美元资产买⼊⽇元的⼲预⾏动期重合，中国⼤陆则延续了⻓期减
持的趋势，⼀年来减少上千亿美元。
⽇本汇市⼲预规模创纪录，美元资产配置受关注
美国财政部 TIC 数据显⽰， 8 ⽉⽇本持有的美国国债规模环⽐ 7 ⽉减少 356 亿美元，降⾄ 1.0683 万
亿美元，仍是美国最⼤的海外 “ 债主 ” 。
据 TIC 报告，⽇本的美债持仓已连续四个⽉下降。此前 6 ⽉、 7 ⽉持仓连续创 2025 年 1 ⽉以来新
低， 8 ⽉进⼀步降⾄ 2024 年 12 ⽉以来最低⽔平。
⽇本持仓持续缩⽔之际，⽇本政府今年多次⼲预外汇市场、试图⽀撑⽇元，市场因此关注汇市
⼲预是否影响⽇本的美元资产配置。
⽇本财务省 5 ⽉ 29 ⽇公布的数据显⽰， 4 ⽉ 28 ⽇⾄ 5 ⽉ 27 ⽇期间，⽇本外汇市场⼲预总额达到
11.73 万亿⽇元。分析⼈⼠认为，⽇本买⼊⽇元、卖出美元的交易可能涉及⼀定规模的美国国债

出售。
此后，⽇美两国还罕⻅地联⼿⼲预汇市。⽇本财务省 8 ⽉ 3 ⽇证实，⽇本政府与美国财政部于 7 ⽉
31 ⽇协调实施了买⼊⽇元的汇率⼲预，以遏制⽇元汇率近期的剧烈波动。这是⽇美两国⾃ 2011
年东⽇本⼤地震后⾸次协调⼲预汇市，也是⾦融危机等特殊时期之外极为罕⻅的联合⾏动。
⽇本财务省随后公布的数据显⽰， 7 ⽉ 30 ⽇⾄ 8 ⽉ 26 ⽇期间，⽇本政府共计投⼊约 15.4 万亿⽇元
⼲预外汇市场，创下单⽉⼲预⾦额新⾼。
⼲预汇市通常需要卖出外汇、买⼊⽇元。由于⽇本外汇储备中持有⼤量美国国债，市场因此关
注，⽇本是否会通过出售美债等美元资产为⼲预⾏动提供资⾦。
不过， TIC 数据并不能直接证明⽇本为⼲预汇市出售了多少美债。持仓余额的变化既受到实际买
卖影响，也可能受到债券价格变化等估值因素影响，不能将⽇本 8 ⽉持仓减少 356 亿美元全部归
因于汇市⼲预。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

中国持仓连降三个⽉  两⽉连创⼗⼋年新低
据 TIC 报告，中国⼤陆 8 ⽉持有的美国国债规模环⽐减少 273 亿美元，降⾄ 5907 亿美元，连续两
个⽉创 2008 年 8 ⽉以来新低，继续位居美国第三⼤海外债主。
截⾄ 8 ⽉，中国⼤陆的美债持仓已连续三个⽉下降，最近 12 个⽉内，有⼗个⽉持仓环⽐下滑。以
TIC ⼀年来的数据计算，截⾄ 8 ⽉末，中国⼤陆所持美国国债相⽐去年同期⼤幅减少 1090 亿美
元。
从更⻓周期看，中国⼤陆持有的美国国债规模已较过去明显下降。 2016 年前后，中国⼤陆持仓
曾超过 1 万亿美元，如今已降⾄约 5900 亿美元，反映出官⽅持仓持续收缩的趋势。
与此同时，市场近期也出现关于中国可能通过第三地持有部分美国债券的讨论。据本周三的报
道，华尔街部分投资者和分析⼈⼠怀疑，中国可能通过加拿⼤等地的⾦融机构持有部分美国债
券。相关讨论与加拿⼤近年来美国国债及相关机构债券持仓增加有关。
但这⼀说法尚⽆确凿证据。加拿⼤持仓上升并不能直接证明其中包含中国转移的资产，也可能

受到其他投资者配置变化的影响。
因此，中国⼤陆持仓持续下滑是 TIC 数据能够确认的趋势，但仅凭公开持仓数据，尚不能断⾔中
国实际持有的全部美国债券规模同步下降。
加拿⼤增持居⾸，⽐利时持仓也⼤增
在 TIC 报告的⼗⼤海外持有美债国家地区中， 8 ⽉只有⽇本和中国的持仓减少。总持仓规模介于
中⽇之间的英国连续两个⽉增持。
数据显⽰，英国持有的美债规模环⽐增加 112 亿美元，升⾄ 1.0095 万亿美元，有纪录以来⾸次突
破 1 万亿美元，连续两个⽉持仓创最⾼纪录，继续稳居 TIC 报告的海外 “ 债主 ” 榜单次席。
加拿⼤和⽐利时的增持幅度更为突出。加拿⼤ 8 ⽉美债持仓环⽐增加 383 亿美元，升⾄约 4646 亿
美元，增幅在美国⼗⼤海外持有国中居⾸，总持仓排名第五。
⽐利时持仓环⽐增加 198 亿美元，升⾄约 4905 亿美元，增幅仅次于加拿⼤，总持仓排名第四。
值得注意的是，市场近期也在讨论第三地托管数据是否完整反映最终投资者的⾝份。美国财政
部的 TIC 数据主要依据托管机构和交易商报告编制，如果外国投资者通过第三国托管机构持有美

国证券，相关资产可能被记在托管地名下，⽽⾮最终所有者所在国家。
因此，单个国家持仓增加并不⼀定完全对应其本国投资者的实际买⼊。尤其在分析中国等⼤型
持有国的美债配置变化时，托管地与最终所有者之间的差异值得留意。
海外私⼈资⾦流⼊占主导，美债需求能否延续
整体来看，中⽇以外的主要持有美债地区 8 ⽉有不同程度的增加。
在美国⼗⼤海外美债持有国家地区中，总持仓第六位的开曼群岛持债⼩幅增加 29 亿美元⾄ 4630
亿美元，第七位的卢森堡持仓增 61 亿美元⾄ 4482 亿美元，第⼋位的法国持仓增加 150 亿美元、
增幅仅次于⽐利时，增⾄ 3634 亿美元，第九位的爱尔兰持仓增 19 亿美元⾄ 3521 亿美元，中国台
湾地区持仓增 23 亿美元⾄ 2984 亿美元，位居第⼗。

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

2578 亿美元，外国官⽅机构净买⼊ 429 亿美元。若进⼀步计⼊短期证券、银⾏资⾦流动等项⽬，
当⽉美国录得 1998 亿美元的净国际资本流⼊。
这些数据表明， 8 ⽉海外资⾦整体仍在流⼊美国⾦融市场，美债需求也有所恢复。不过，海外资
⾦回流与主要官⽅持有国减持并不⽭盾：不同投资者的配置策略可能不同，私⼈投资者的买⼊
也可以抵消部分官⽅机构的减持。
对美债市场⽽⾔，后续值得关注的，⼀⽅⾯是海外投资者净买⼊能否持续，另⼀⽅⾯是⽇本汇
市⼲预是否继续影响其美元资产配置，以及中国⼤陆官⽅持仓下降在多⼤程度上反映实际减
持。
海外资⾦回流为美债需求提供了⽀撑，但⽇本、中国继续减持，也意味着不能仅凭单⽉总持仓

回升，就认定所有主要海外持有者对美国国债的配置意愿都已同步改善。

Document 240: First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion, September quarter 2026 (2026-10-16).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#businessconditions #newzealand
First Impressions: NZIER Quarterly Survey of Business Opinion,
September quarter 2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
08:35 October 06 2026 Share
Businesses are feeling more optimistic about the months ahead, but current conditions
remain tough for many industries. Cost pressures eased off a little compared to last quarter.
Key results (seasonally adjusted)
General business situation: +40 (Prev: +14)

### Page 2

Trading activity, past three months: -1 (Prev: +1) 
Trading activity, next three months: +15 (Prev: +11) 
Average selling prices, past three months: +27 (Prev: +37) 
Average selling prices, next three months: +45 (Prev: +51)

The NZIER’s quarterly survey of business opinion showed a strong lift in general confidence in
September, despite resurgent fuel prices and the uncertainty around the looming election. Firms were
more confident about their prospects for the next few months, even though their recent performance
remained rough in many industries. 
The survey was held over a relatively compressed period (9 to 28 September), with around 40% of
responses coming back on the first day. Later responses were less confident, as the resurgence in
world oil prices was finally reflected in prices at the pump over the course of the month. 
General business sentiment rose to a net 40% positive compared to a net 14% in June, returning to
around the levels seen in the December quarter last year (before the Middle East conflict). However,
the own-activity measures, which tend to have a closer correspondence with GDP growth, were more
subdued. Trading activity over the last three months was slightly weaker than in June, while
expectations for the quarter ahead saw a more modest four-point increase to +15%. 
Other forward-looking measures were also up for the quarter. Hiring intentions rose strongly to a net
23% positive (although past employment was down). Notably, firms continue to report that workers are
becoming harder to find, despite the ongoing rise in unemployment over the last couple of years. That
said, the proportion of firms citing labour as a factor limiting output declined this quarter. Investment
intentions picked up, more so for plant and machinery than for buildings. 
The results were mixed across industries. Manufacturing and services were more upbeat for the
months ahead but reported substantially weaker performance over the last quarter. In contrast, builders
and merchants reported both stronger past results and greater optimism about the near-term outlook. 
The pricing measures in the survey were down compared to June – not entirely surprising as fuel
prices were lower on average through the September quarter compared to the June quarter. The
notable exception was merchants, who expected both higher costs and prices for the quarter ahead. 
The implications for the RBNZ ahead of its 28 October policy review are unclear – there is certainly no
smoking gun in this report that would demand that the RBNZ abandon the “pause” strategy
communicated at the September MPS. Optimism among businesses remains high despite the obvious
headwinds, but there is still a wide gap between hopes and reality. The pricing measures have eased
compared to last quarter, but that may simply confirm the RBNZ’s expectation that annual inflation will
drop from the 4.1% recorded last quarter as fuel prices eased. The September quarter CPI, released
on 22 October, may be more informative in that respect. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

### Page 1

IEA 成员国同意完成 3 ⽉⽯油储备释放计划中尚未落地的 1 亿桶配额，并承诺优先 “ 尽快 ” 释放
其中 2000 万桶柴油。但这批储量本质上是对既有承诺的加速兑现，摩根⼤通和欧盟官员均
明确表⽰并⾮新增⼲预。与此同时，霍尔⽊兹海峡局势持续紧张， WTI 本周累计上涨约
6% ，储备释放对油价的压制效果⼗分有限。
摘要：
国际能源署（ IEA ）成员国同意完成 3 ⽉⽯油储备释放计划中尚未落地的 1 亿桶配额，并承诺 " 尽
快 " 释放其中 2000 万桶柴油。然⽽，这批储备本质上是对既有承诺的加速兑现，⽽⾮新⼀轮⼲预
⾏动。
据彭博报道， IEA 理事会于当地时间 10 ⽉ 15 ⽇周四召开会议，会后发表声明称，成员国将加快分
配剩余储量，包括 " 尽快 " 释放 2000 万桶柴油。 IEA 同时指出，由于部分成员国释放量超出初始承
诺，最终实际释放总量将超过 3 ⽉宣布的 4 亿桶上限。
油价对此反应有限。 WTI 原油本周累计上涨约 6% ，周四单⽇涨幅达 3% ，⽬前交投于每桶 98 美元

下⽅；布伦特原油则稳定在每桶 106 美元附近。霍尔⽊兹海峡局势持续紧张，仍是市场主要上⾏
⻛险来源。
1 亿桶是旧账
IEA 于 2026 年 3 ⽉宣布释放 4 亿桶⽯油储备，以应对伊朗战争引发的供应中断。截⾄⽬前，成员
国已释放 3.3 亿桶，此次 1 亿桶系补⾜剩余缺⼝。
此前，摩根⼤通分析师明确指出，这批储备释放 " 在很⼤程度上是加速兑现此前已有承诺，⽽⾮
代表规模达 1 亿桶的全新⼲预⾏动 " 。
欧盟委员会发⾔⼈亦表态称，计划中的储备释放 " 必须在 3 ⽉批准的总量范围内推进 " 。德国经济
部确认将参与释放，但未披露任何新增规模。
美国施压，欧洲划定边界
此次会议背后，美欧之间存在明显分歧。

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂

据彭博报道，美国曾⾮正式游说追加释放，以压制国内飙升的柴油价格，但在本次会议上并未
提出正式提案。知情⼈⼠透露，欧洲官员对于此时追加释放持怀疑态度。
美国能源部在声明中表⽰， " 以柴油为重点的进⼀步⾏动正在审查和推进中 " 。
特朗普政府措辞更为强硬。能源部声明称： " 特朗普政府持续要求欧洲国家履⾏此前承诺，以减
少全球能源市场供应中断、帮助压低能源价格。继特朗普总统在 G7 上发出呼吁后，所有⾝为 IEA
成员的欧洲国家均已承诺⽴即完成 3 ⽉承诺并释放额外桶量。 "
IEA ⽅⾯拒绝就声明作进⼀步说明。
在柴油问题上，欧盟成员国内部讨论显⽰，⽅向可⾏但规模预计有限。截⾄⽬前，尚⽆欧盟成
员国明确承诺在 G7 设定的 20 天窗⼝内额外释放柴油库存。波兰能源部⻓⽶沃什 · 莫蒂卡表⽰，若

各⽅达成共识，波兰已准备好释放⽯油和柴油库存。法国和意⼤利可能考虑在 3 ⽉承诺基础上进
⼀步释放部分柴油，但尚未作出最终决定。
柴油危机是核⼼压⼒点
IEA 此次将柴油单独列出，折射出当前市场最紧迫的⽭盾所在。
伊朗战争与乌克兰冲突导致炼油设施受损、油轮航运受阻，柴油价格在多个市场创下历史新
⾼。据彭博报道，特朗普曾警告，若 G7 国家不向市场投放更多柴油，他可能禁⽌美国柴油出
⼝，并已宣布与莫斯科达成供应协议。
IEA 声明显⽰，成员国⽬前持有的公共应急⽯油库存约为 11 亿桶，其中柴油超过 2 亿桶。
美国能源信息署（ EIA ）数据显⽰，上周美国原油库存下降 860 万桶，为今年 2 ⽉以来最⼤单周降
幅，馏分油库存亦减少 250 万桶，降⾄季节性新低。
霍尔⽊兹⻛险持续升温
储备释放的市场效果，正被地缘局势的不确定性所抵消。

命卫队投射物击中。这艘悬挂新加坡旗帜的 GFS Pride 在被拖往岸边途中沉没。

尽管如此，过去 24 ⼩时内仍有逾 40 艘船只通过霍尔⽊兹海峡，显⽰航运并未完全中断。

Makalot Industrial  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 Sales In-Line; Attractive
Entry Point; Keep OW
September sales reached NT$2.93bn (+24% YoY/−18% MoM), driven by shipment
growth of 22% YoY, ASP down 1%, and the balance from FX. Management noted
that shipments worth NT$80mn were delayed to next month due to volatile
shipping schedules.
3Q26 sales (+7% YoY) were broadly in line with both our estimates and consensus.
Shipments rose 1% (slightly below expectations due to shipment delays and order

cherry-picking), ASP was flat, and FX contributed +6%. Based on our simulations,
July and August GPM should exceed 3Q26 guidance of 24%.
Makalot kept 4Q26 guidance unchanged, with shipments expected to grow at a low-
single-digit YoY rate, ASP up 5–7% (supported by cherry-picking higher-ASP, higher-
profit orders), and GPM at 26% (-0.7ppt YoY).
We continue to expect Makalot's 2026 earnings to return close to peak levels. Our
net income forecast of NT$3.9bn is back to the 2023 level and only a low-single-

digit percentage below the record achieved in 2024. We believe macro uncertainties
are already priced in and view the current level as an attractive entry point, with
valuation at 11.3x our 2H26–1H27 EPS estimate and a 7.9% cash yield based on our
projected 2026 dividend.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Makalot Industrial (1477.TW)
Base case value, derived by applying a multiple of 17x to our 2026 EPS estimate. Our target
multiple is below its average P/E ratio in the prior up-cycle of 21x (after re-rating in 2H23), and
is within the stock's 10-year historical 9-29x P/E range. We view this as reasonable, with 2026e
earnings growth of 8% seen outperforming apparel and footwear peers with flat to single-
digit growth.

Risks to Upside
n New non-US customer wins
n Faster-than-expected inventory digestion
n ASP and margin expansion via more complex garments
n Better US GDP growth
n Faster order reallocation owing to trade tension
Risks to Downside
n Deterioration in the macro environment
n Drastic appreciation of TWD
n Pricing competition initiated from competitors
n Tariff increases on apparel exports from Vietnam or Cambodia

Document 316: The end of the safety net_ Life without the macroeconomic stabilisers (2026-09-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/The end of the safety net_ Life without the macroeconomic stabilisers (2026-09-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

The end of the safety net:  
Life without the macroeconomic stabilisers

1 
Authors 
Henry Allen 
Macro Strategist

28 September 2026 
For several decades, financial markets and the wider economy benefited from an implicit 
safety net. If a slowdown or a downturn arrived, there was a monetary put from central 
banks, and a fiscal put from governments. The monetary put would see interest rates 
slashed and liquidity injected via QE. The fiscal put would see governments run larger 
deficits to cushion any downturn. These policy stabilisers smoothed out the business 
cycle, and supported asset valuations too. 
These policy interventions were increasingly normalised from the late-1980s, and became 
more aggressive with each passing cycle. This reached its peak during the Covid-19 
pandemic, when governments ran fiscal deficits unprecedented in peacetime, whilst 
many central banks expanded their balance sheets with huge asset purchases. 
But after an era of ever-bigger interventions, these old stabilisers now face major 
obstacles. On the fiscal side, policy is constrained today by sovereign yields at multi-year 
highs, alongside the highest debt-to-GDP ratios in several decades. Then on the monetary 
side, policy is constrained by persistent inflation that’s increasingly supply-driven. 
This represents a big shift from the last few decades, but it does echo previous regimes 
where the macroeconomic stabilisers were either blunted or left unused. A good example 
is the 1970s experience, when stagflation and supply shocks posed a dilemma to 
traditional policy tools. Even further back, the pre-WWI environment of the classical gold 
standard and balanced budget orthodoxy saw governments play a very different and more 
limited role in macroeconomic stabilisation. 
Looking forward, this environment means policymakers will have a much more difficult 
time stabilising the business cycle in the coming years. So absent a sharp fall in yields or a 
collapse in inflation, higher macro volatility will be here to stay.

### Page 2

28 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
1. Background: How the stabilisers used to work.
Economic policymakers have long used interventions to stabilise output. This
became increasingly fashionable after WWII, when Keynesian economics
established a more active use for fiscal policy to stimulate the economy in a
downturn. This meant budget deficits became a more regular feature of life, rather
than used solely for wartime emergencies.

It was from the late 1980s, however, that the extent of policy interventions became
increasingly sizeable. Indeed, it was here that the origins of a central bank “put” for
financial markets began, in the crisis of Black Monday in 1987. That was a huge
stock market crash, where the S&P 500 fell over 20% in a single day, marking the
biggest daily decline in its entire history. The size of the falls even went beyond the
Great Depression, raising fears of wider contagion, particularly if it were to spark a

liquidity crisis that caused financial institutions to fail. However, the Federal
Reserve responded by cutting rates and providing liquidity, offering a
macroeconomic stabiliser against the prospect of wider economic stress. This
offered support against a wider downturn, and the US economic expansion
continued until 1990.
Whilst the genesis of a central bank “put” came in 1987, the idea gained further
traction in the late-1990s, with the Asian financial crisis in 1997, followed by the

Russian financial crisis in 1998. Fears of an economic contraction mounted,
particularly after issues at Long-Term Capital Management (LTCM) raised concerns
that the US wasn’t immune from these global pressures. So once again, the Fed
delivered three 25bp rate cuts pre-emptively, seeking to support the economy.
A few years later, the bursting of the dot com bubble in early 2000 set the stage for
yet further stimulus. As equity markets declined, the Fed began to cut rates from

early 2001, ultimately delivering 475bps of cuts that year that took rates from 6.5%
to just 1.75%. That was followed up by further rate cuts in 2002 and 2003, leaving
rates at 1% by mid-2003 into 2004. Moreover, the Bush tax cuts were also delivered
in 2001 and 2003, acting as a fiscal stimulus as the wider US economy entered
recession.
In hindsight, these moves seem modest compared to what then happened in 2008,
when the Global Financial Crisis led to even bigger policy interventions. Suddenly,

the world faced the biggest economic contraction since the Great Depression, with
genuine fears that a repeat might happen. To avoid this, policymakers launched into
the biggest economic stimulus ever seen in a peacetime environment. That
included massive fiscal stimulus, with the US federal deficit at 9.8% of GDP in 2008,
the highest since 1945. Moreover, central banks slashed rates to the zero lower
bound, and many like the Federal Reserve launched into Quantitative Easing

programmes, purchasing assets to ease financial conditions even more. Over the
coming years, many began pledging to keep rates low well into the future,
pioneering the concept of forward guidance.
These levels of policy intervention weren’t confined to the immediate crisis
however. In the subsequent years, active attempts were made to prolong and
extend the business cycle, particularly as the 2010s became an era of low inflation
and low interest rates. In this environment, borrowing was historically cheap, and

low inflation meant central banks could cut rates without seeing a big overshoot of
their targets. Indeed, both the Fed and the ECB undershot their 2% inflation target
on average in the 2010s, and the ECB took their own deposit rate into negative
territory in 2014.

By 2019, the year before the pandemic, the intellectual climate had continued to
move in the direction of more policy intervention. Government bond yields hit new

lows, with Germany’s 10-year bund yield at -0.7% in summer 2019. This
environment of negative yields meant borrowing was basically a “free lunch”, and
that summer also saw the Fed begin a 75bp easing cycle, as inflation was below
their target and they wanted to sustain the expansion.
The peak of this policy interventionism was seen with the Covid-19 pandemic in
2020. Just as in 2008, the world was facing a huge and sudden economic
contraction. Moreover, the consensus among policymakers was that they could

have done more to speed up the recovery after 2008, potentially avoiding years of
very high unemployment. As such, 2020 saw the most aggressive economic
stimulus packages ever seen in a peacetime environment, well above those in 2008.
Central banks immediately slashed rates back to the zero lower bound, pledged to
keep rates low for years to come, and launched into major QE programmes again.
Then on the fiscal side, record peacetime deficits were run. Indeed, the US federal

deficit of 14.5% of GDP in 2020 was the highest since 1945.

### Page 4

28 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG
During the pandemic period, many initially expected these interventions to remain
for years to come. After all, in mid-2020, financial markets continued to price a
continuation of the low inflation and low interest rates of the 2010s. This belief was
clear on several fronts, and the 10-year Treasury yield closed at just 0.51% in August
2020. Meanwhile, the Federal Reserve even adjusted its own framework to allow for

average inflation targeting, i.e. permitting an overshoot of the inflation target after
periods of an undershoot. This reflected the consensus view that inflation was
unlikely to be a serious threat.
But as we now know, inflation returned in a serious way after the pandemic, forcing
central banks into rate hikes. Meanwhile, public debt-to-GDP ratios rose
significantly thanks to the pandemic stimulus, helping to push up sovereign bond
yields too. In other words, many of the usual policy tools of the previous few

decades were either blunted or no longer available.
2. The Monetary Constraint: High inflation means the central
bank put isn’t available
In the pre-2020 world described above, central banks regularly grappled with how
to generate enough inflation. In part, this was thanks to several structural forces
that put downward pressure on global prices. For instance, deepening
globalisation acted as a positive supply shock that kept costs down. Meanwhile, the

expansion of the global labour force in these years also boosted the supply side. In
other words, inflation was low and central banks generally had the space to cut
interest rates when they needed to.
But since the pandemic, we have entered a very different environment of above-
target inflation across many of the world’s biggest economies. Initially, that was
caused by a huge wave of pent-up demand after the pandemic, alongside
disruption to supply chains and labour markets. But the subsequent years also

brought further shocks. Those included Russia’s invasion of Ukraine in early 2022,
driving a big spike in European energy prices, and pushing Euro Area inflation above
10% briefly. In 2026, the closure of the Strait of Hormuz then acted as a fresh energy
shock, pushing inflation further away from target and contributing to a fresh
tightening cycle from the major central banks.
Figure 4: In the 2020s, inflation has generally been above central bank targets

(PCE inflation for the US, HICP inflation for the Euro Area)

### Page 5

28 September 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
Alongside those shocks, the structural forces keeping inflation down have gone
into reverse. For instance, demographic trends have seen working-age populations
shrink in many places. Meanwhile, the trend towards deeper globalisation has
stalled, and the focus has turned to near-shoring and supply-chain resilience, rather
than locating production in the cheapest place. Simultaneously, other trends like

the push for higher defence spending are also adding to demand-side pressures.
Because of all this, the 2020s so far have brought persistently above-target inflation,
and central bankers have found it much more difficult to ease policy in response to
growth wobbles or financial stability fears.
This initially became apparent in 2022, amidst the biggest inflation wave for several
decades. Central banks had to keep hiking rates, even in the face of slowing growth

and financial market stress. Indeed, despite the S&P 500 being in bear market
territory by mid-2022, the Federal Reserve still launched into a series of 75bp hikes,
the biggest hikes since the 1990s. Then in 2023, despite the collapse of Silicon
Valley Bank and fears of wider financial contagion, the Fed and ECB still continued
with their rate hikes to combat inflation.
That tightening cycle in 2022-23 was a significant moment, because high inflation

meant monetary policy could not simply act as a stabiliser against growth or
financial stress. After all, policymakers realised that if they didn’t act to tackle high
inflation, they risked a repeat of the 1970s and the potential for even harsher
monetary tightening to get inflation under control again.
Today in 2026, this is a familiar problem again. We are in a globally synchronised
hiking cycle, with the Fed, ECB and Bank of Japan all having delivered rate hikes.

So far at least, global growth has been robust and financial conditions remain
accommodative by historical standards. But because inflation remains clearly
above target, central banks don’t have the space they did in the pre-pandemic world
to simply cut rates at the first sign of difficulty. There is still an inflation mandate to
be achieved, and given we have now seen multiple years of elevated inflation, there
is a much higher risk of inflation expectations becoming unanchored in that

context, particularly if another shock occurred.
3. The Fiscal Constraint: High yields and high public debt
mean fiscal stimulus isn’t easily available either
Just as monetary policy is constrained by high inflation, fiscal policy today is also
constrained by higher yields and higher public debt. This is a big transition from the
world of 1980-2020, when yields were generally falling throughout. Indeed, despite
all the fiscal stimulus around the GFC, the continuous decline in yields over the

2010s meant that debt dynamics remained manageable, because lower yields
helped to offset the impact of higher debt.
Since the pandemic however, we’ve been in a very different world, as the dual
forces of higher debt and higher yields have made debt dynamics far more
unfavourable around the world. And this isn’t just a nominal story, as real yields
have also risen to multi-year highs as well.

In essence, there are 4 new constraints around fiscal stimulus.
First, is the significant rise in global yields. By definition, the cost of incurring debt
has grown, in both nominal and real terms. Relative to the pre-pandemic baseline

in early 2020, the 10-year US Treasury yield has risen from 1.9% to 5.2%. Similarly
in Germany, the 10-year bund yield has risen from -0.2% to 3.6%. Moreover, with
yields already elevated, any further borrowing risks crowding out the private sector,
and further dampening growth. And for governments, interest spending has
ramped up significantly. For instance, US net interest spending as a share of GDP
exceeded its recent peak from the early 1990s in the most recent fiscal year, and the

CBO forecast this is set to continue.
Figure 6: US Net Interest Costs (% of GDP) with CBO Forecasts
